# Code map: default.xbe

An index of the default.xbe functions and addresses the project relies on: what each one does,
how sure we are, and where it's used or written up. Look here before tracing a function; it may
be done already. Addresses are virtual addresses in the US `default.xbe` (base 0x10000; `.text`
0x12000-0x3118b0, `.rdata` from 0x386340, `.data` from 0x3b5b00).

* `FUN_0022c4d0` is an address where Ghidra made a function: its C is in
  `decompiled/xbe/ghidra/c/` (`python tools/fn.py 22c4d0` prints it). `0x22d0b0` is code Ghidra
  didn't split out: read it in `decompiled/xbe/default.text.asm`. Data (tables, vtables,
  globals) is written `0x3bd9d4`; `tools/xbe_peek.py f|i|x ADDR` reads values. Search the page
  for an address's hex digits without `0x` or leading zeros (`22c4d0`, `7c720`) to find it in
  either form.
* `bit N` is a bit index (bit 0 is mask 1, bit 3 is mask 8); `flag 0x40` is a mask.
* A row covers a function; the places inside it that a page cites, and the constants it reads,
  are in that row. The constants also have their own table at the end.
* Name tables are `{length, name, value}` records. Their address here is where the game's text
  parser starts reading: the first name pointer, 4 bytes past the first record.
* Offsets: `char+0x490` is a character object's field, `type+0x220` its character type's
  (`[char+0xc]`), `skill+0x1c` its skill object's (`[char+0x5a4]`), `weapon+0x218` a weapon
  object's, `data+0xd0` the weapon data a weapon points at from +0x228.

**How sure** (the third column):
* **code**: read from the disassembly or Ghidra's C (static, not run).
* **live**: also checked in the running game (xemu's gdb stub, or a take that matches it).
* **data**: read from the game's data files (`decompiled/xml/`), with the code that uses it.
* **inferred**: deduced from its use, the data or the footage, not read line by line.
* **guess**: a reading nobody has checked.

Brackets narrow a word: the part that is only inferred, or the source's own rating (medium,
low).

**Where** (the fourth column): a docs page and its section, a source file under `viewer/src/`
(`play_shots.rs` for `src/bin/play_shots.rs`), a ticket. `todo/35` leads, spec or notes are the
research in the git-ignored `todo/35-*/` folder; `todo/damage-factor` is the lead's code
question in `todo/damage-factor/answer.md`. "This page" means it was read for this index.

**Adding a row.** Put it in its area's table, at its address. One line: what it does, with the
game's own names (enum names, strings, attribute names); how sure; where it's used. When a row
is wrong, correct it and say so in your report. When two places disagree, read the code, record
what it does in the row and say which place was wrong.

STANDARD -4, HARD 0, BRUTAL 4. The default game flags hold it at -4: `common/default-gameflags.xml` `<campaign>` lists it, the decoder naming its hash h_1a98de0a `shadoon-look` (a collision with `difficulty-setting`), so a session built from them reads STANDARD. In a match it gives 0: read live at 0x12566d in #129's mp1 deathmatch takes (todo/129-shrikes/notes.md B2, takes 35, 36, 38, 39: each hit the roll x the bone's Damage, no x1.4), as #85 take29 (sdm_e34, a barrel at x1 where the campaign's takes are x0.6) and #146 takes 01-03 (sdm_e13, lava at exactly its 30 per entry) had it. The step itself (0x125600) has no mode check, and none of the setter's (0x7c750) callers (SELECT DIFFICULTY, RESUME LAST, QUICK CAMPAIGN, the system-link join at 0x1eef81) is on a split-screen match's start, so a match's slot evidently lacks the flag; which slot it reads isn't traced, and system-link play (whose join calls the setter) is untested. The demo: STANDARD on the missions and the test map, 0 on the multiplayer maps. | code (n = -4 in the campaign: read live, #129; 0 in a split-screen match: read live, mechanism untraced) | todo/damage-factor; play.md § Damage; `play_damage.rs` (`Difficulty::for_session`) |

## Effects / ALE

Materials and shaders are here too.

| Address | What it does | How sure | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| `FUN_0001d0c0` | Builds a frame from a direction (y up for a level one): the explosion turned along a missile's way (FUN_0022cbe0), a decal's frame (FUN_0022ca20). The frame's z column is -d (so -z along d), x = up x z, y = z x x; the up hint is world y, or world z when abs(d.y) > 0.9848. The MIRV split builds its frame with it from the parent's velocity. | code | play.md § Missiles; todo/73 answer-round4 §Q2; `play_missile.rs` (`split`) |
| `0x64e30` | Spawn of the cube emitter class e0ca2222 (no Ghidra function): a random point in the box (+0xfc, +0xf8, +0xf4) and a direction within the spreads; with node +0x4c bit 0 (h_e2999ffd, `CubeEmitter_OffsetHeight` in the parameter table at 0x40e390) the box's height runs from the emitter up (0x64ee2). Getter / setter 0x64c30 / 0x64c60. The demo reads h_e2999ffd as its own ATTACHED (`ale_fx.rs`), which this contradicts. | code | todo/73 answer-round4 §Q5 |
| `0x65320` | ALE emitter constructor: sets the curve base values (100 a second, 2 s), the editor's defaults, unused once a curve has keys. | code | this page |
| `FUN_00065640` | ALE emitter instance constructor (vtable 0x3a3318, whose +0x10 is FUN_00065800 and +0x8c the LOD setter 0x66a20): its time +0x80 and last batch's time +0xa8 = 0, LOD +0x8c = 1.0, and the first-update flag +0xb4 = 1, the only place it's set: InitialParticles come once. | code | play.md § Missiles; `ale_fx.rs` (`game_count`) |
| `FUN_00065800` | ALE emitter update. Count = trunc(elapsed x Emitter_Frequency), + InitialParticles on the first update, + the EmitCount keys in [t_last, t], then trunc(Emitter_LODCurve(LOD +0x8c) x count) (0x659ed-0x65a12). t_last is held while the count is 0 and the frequency isn't; on emitting, t_last = t and the fraction is lost. Runs once a game frame: cut each update, its count depends on the step. Node fields: +0x30 Frequency, +0x34 InitLifeSpan, +0x38 Pressure, +0x3c EmitCount, +0x40 VelocityApproach, +0x44 InitialParticles, +0x48 LODCurve. | code | todo/73 answer-round3 §3.2; play.md § Missiles; `ale_fx.rs` (`game_count`, `EMIT_LOD`, `GAME_FRAME`) |
